### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ TOPIX 啟動大規模改革：日本交易所集團 10 月 7 日公布 TOPIX 調整結果，符合新制納入標
準的成分股將由 9 月底的 1,636 檔降至 986 檔，另有 35 檔股票新納入。現有成分股中，共
有 683 檔未符合流動性及浮動市值等標準，這些股票將自 2026 年 10 月底起逐步降低權
重，並於 2027 年 10 月重新評估，屆時仍未達標者將於 2028 年 7 月底降至零。此次改革有
助於改善 TOPIX 成分股的流動性及投資價值，並促使企業更加重視市場評價。

◼ 巴西總統選舉波索納洛首輪領先魯拉：巴西最高選舉法院 10 月 5 日公布首輪投票結果，參
議員弗拉維奧・波索納洛取得 47.03%的有效票，現任總統魯拉獲得 45.16%，兩人均未取得
過半票數，將於 10 月 25 日進行第二輪決選。若最終由波索納洛當選將相較有利於巴西股市
表現。
◼ IEA 支持加速釋出既有儲油：國際能源署（IEA）10 月 7 日表示，會員國支持加快釋出 3 月
已承諾提供的儲油，並將優先增加柴油供應。截至目前，會員國已釋出約 3.25 億桶儲油；若
剩餘承諾全數執行，可再向市場提供約 1 億桶供應，有助緩解運輸業與農業面臨的燃料成本
壓力。隨各國持續釋出戰略儲備石油，將有利於在短期內壓抑全球油價進一步上行。
市場展望
◼ 標普 500 獲利預估連續上修，資訊科技為主要推升來源：標普 500 企業獲利預估連續兩季上
修，其中資訊科技產業為主要改善來源，反映 AI、雲端運算及資料中心需求仍然強勁。雖然
美國資料中心仍面臨電力與基礎設施瓶頸，但建置規模持續擴大，AI 投資也逐步延伸至記憶

體、網路設備、資安及企業應用等領域。因此，預期科技類股的獲利動能仍具支撐，具 AI 供
應鏈優勢的台股與日股亦可望延續相關成長動能。
◼ 中天期債券可望兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府與企業發債供給維持高
檔，可能使市場利率維持在較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企業獲利、
現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，較高的美國公債基準利率也使其利息收益維持一定水準。短天
期債券面臨利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較易受到公債供給及利率波動影響，因
此中天期美國公債及投資級債可望在息收與利率風險之間維持較為平衡的表現。

## Document 117: 261015_ms_TSMC

Source PDF: all report pdf/261015_ms_TSMC.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

M
Idea
TSMC  | Asia Pacific
No slowdown of AI semi
demand; reiterate OW
TSMC is further expanding capacity after considering other
constraints, such as data center build, power and other
components supply.  We think the risk of over-capacity is small,
and stay OW.
TSMC sees robust AI semi demand: Despite CSP power shortages and rising
financing costs, TSMC sees demand far above its capacity. It sees no signs of a
slowdown, which may ease some market concerns. The company did not update its

AI semi CAGR guidance (55-60%), saying it is not meaningful as  the demand is
expanding rapidly.
Comments on  wafer foundry partnership (TeraFab) and competition (Intel): For
overseas fabs, TSMC is evaluating a TeraFab partnership, but TSMC must run the
partner fab. TSMC is also considering other fab campus locations, including Texas.
We asked management about IDM outsourcing from Intel and implications for A14
capacity demand. Management said it would try to be fair to all customers. On

competition, TSMC would expand capacity and “leave no food on the table” for
competitors.
On advanced packaging, we asked whether TSMC would help customers using
Intel EMIB-T advanced packaging: The company is confident about 9x and 14x
reticle size chip support. TSMC’s principle is to help customers ramp products
smoothly, so we take it as no objection if Google/MediaTek needs to adopt CoWoS/
CoPoS in future TPU versions, as we articulated in MediaTek: TSMC CoWoS support

eases MediaTek's 2028 TPU production concerns
We raise 2027/28 capex estimates to factor in 2nm and 1.4nm expansion: The
company maintained its 2026 capex guidance (US$60-64bn) and didn’t provide
next-year capex guidance. That said, it  mentioned more than a doubling of 2nm
capacity build by 2027.  Our initial cross checks with equipment venders suggest
EUV tool bookings double in 2027 (vs. 2026), followed by 25-30% growth in 2028.
We therefore raise our 2027/28 capex assumption to US$83/92bn.

3Q26 results review – margin miss, but revenue upside: Pease see Exhibit 1  and
Exhibit 2  for our earnings review. We lower our 2026 and 2027 margin
assumptions,  offset by higher revenue. We maintain our price target at NT$3,088
and reiterate our OW rating.

TSMC 3Q26 earnings and 4Q26 guidance  review
3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance review – margin miss, but revenue upside: 3Q26
margin of 66.4% misses our forecast of 67.5%, but was largely in-line with consensus,
given the margin dilution of 2nm node and overseas operation dilution.
The 4Q26 margin midpoint of 66% shows continued pressure. TSMC guides for 4Q26
revenue to grow 14% Q/Q at the midpoint on very strong demand, in line with our 10-15%

Q/Q expectation. Full-year revenue guidance is raised to 44% Y/Y, also similar to our
preview.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
TSMC: Earnings estimate revisions
Our earnings forecasts decline 3% for 2026,  3% for  2027 and is largely unchanged  for
2028: We lower our 2026/27 earnings forecast mostly due to the margin miss of 3Q26
results and weaker than our initial expectation of 4Q26 margin guidance, and also the
wider overseas fab margin dilution into 2027. We slightly raise the revenue assumption for
2027/2028 as TSMC suggested it would speed up the 3nm/2nm capacity build, but that

also comes with larger depreciation and margin pressure when new production ramps.

Our PT is unchanged at NT$3,088. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027

EPS forecast. TSMC currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of
16.5x since 2018. We find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an
intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.

Our bull and bear case scenario values remain at NT$3,720 and NT$1,700, respectively.

further along with node migration.

foundry services with some gross margin
expansion: 1) Global semi revenue CAGR is

share in 16nm, 7nm, 5nm, 3nm, and 2nm. 3)

while capex per k is more demanding than
for 7nm and 5nm.

competition. 5) Demand for 2nm is less than

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

## Document 125: 261018_ms_OCP-takeaways

Source PDF: all report pdf/261018_ms_OCP-takeaways.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 23

### Page 1

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
CPO/NPO and micro     VCSEL based   optical solutions are the
focus for networking, while HVDC takes center stage for power.
Higher bandwidth and better power efficiency: In the networking space, the
migration into higher bandwidth continues, as players like Accton and Celestica
showcased their 1.6T network switches. We think a bigger theme is the effort to
improve the power efficiency of current mainstream pluggable optical solutions.

First off, one well-discussed approach is the adoption CPO or NPO, and it seems the
latter is gaining more attention due to the technical challenges in the former. Spec
standardization, such as CPX connectors, could further accelerate the proliferation,
in our view. Secondly, copper could still help, as Credo has its 1.6T AEC cables for
inter-tray transmission while many other vendors (Amphenol, TE Connectivity,
Molex, etc.) have their 448G CPC solutions for in-tray transmission. Lastly, the micro

VCSEL based optical modules from Credo and ALAB represent another power
efficient solution (12W/end at 1,6T), which has reliability comparable to copper
(100M hours of MTBF/MTBI) for a transmission distance of 50-100m. We believe
this micro VCSEL based solution could potentially replace the AEC for shorter
distance transmission when copper hits the physical limit in the medium term and
be integrated with the CPO architecture in the longer term. In sum, we believe the

above-mentioned solutions are likely to co-exist for different use cases.
800VDC for power delivery taking the center stage: While the Vera Rubin NVL72
rack already has the in-rack power shelf reference design (110KW/shelf at 3+1
redundancy), Nvidia unveiled another reference design this time, using one 660KW
800VDC power rack to support two Vera Rubin NVL72 racks. Moreover, we also
saw a few ±400VDC reference designs as well. Our conversations with companies

on the exhibition floor indicate that the HVDC adoption should start from late
2026, but likely at a small scale extending into 2027. The next checkpoint will be
Vera Rubin Ultra racks, where increasing power consumption and more hardware
devices for inter-rack scale-up network might take up more pace on the rack, though
the liquid cooled power shelf  should ease the space budget a bit. We believe both
solutions are progressing and will closely monitor the dynamics.

Stock picks: We believe that Bizlink (3665.TW) should be a beneficiary of the
proliferation of the micro VCSEL based optical solution, as it is the assembly partner
for Credo and potentially others as well. Chroma (2360.TW) should see drivers
from power testing, CPU/GPU/TPU SL T/FT, and photonics testing (pluggable/
CPO)tools. Delta( (2308.TW) and Lite-On (2301.TW) should continue to grow with
the increasing power consumption of AI clusters, but more meaningful growth

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

2026 OCP Global Summit
Networking dealing with higher bandwidth and better power
efficiency
1.6T network switch and 1.6T transceiver/AEC
CPO remains one of the focus in the networking space during the OCP Global Summit.
Edgecore and  Celestica showcased its 1.6T network switches based on Tomahawk 6 ASIC,
both for the regular model and the CPO model. Moreover, Edgecore also had its OCS
switch model in the booth, which is based on its own LCOS technology. These

demonstrate that both have the capability to accommodate the customer demand for
network switches based on the optical architecture in the future.
On the cable side, Credo showcased its 1.6T AEC, but did not comment on the roadmap of
its 3.2T AEC. Our checks in the exhibition with industry players indicate that the 3.2T AEC
might be a very challenging project, though efforts are still in place.

CPX and NPX all marching forward
Though CPO remains as the holy grail in the space, its yield issue and lower serviceability
still present challenges to the wider adoption in the near term. We believe that the efforts
will continue, but the industry is simultaneously pursuing other more practical solutions

to sort out the power consumption issue of the existing pluggable transceivers,  including
NPO (near-packaged optics), CPC (co-packaged copper) and NPC (near-packaged copper)
Compared to CPO, NPO compromises on the power consumption, but in exchange it eases
the stringent requirement on alignment and serviceability. Given its better technology
readiness and more mature supply chain, we think it might see good traction in the
coming years.

CPC is also becoming a popular design in the networking space. While the 448G server
copper lane is limited to less than 1m of effective transmission distance, it still fits well
into the use case of intra tray interconnects. We have seen many suppliers showcase the
adoption of CPC for between the front panel and chip.

VCSEL based optical solutions gaining more attention
In addition to CPO/NPO/CPC/NPC designs, we see the micro VCSEL based optical
solutions are gaining more attention too. The value proposition is to leverage the wide
and slow architecture for a relatively short transmission distance of 50-100m. It can
achieve 1-2 pJ/bit of power consumption vs. 5-10pJ/bit for CPO and NPO architecture. The

current generation can enable 32Gbps of bandwidth per lane and the 100-200Gbps of
bandwidth per lane is in the roadmap.

12W/end to support 50m of transmission distance with the copper level reliability (100m

hours of MTBF/MTBI)

into the optical space, vs. current concentration on copper.

In sum, different solutions of data transmission are emerging, trying to improve the power
consumption of the currently mainstream pluggable transceivers. We believe that these
solutions will probably co exist for a while, as different use case could need different
combination of cost and benefits.

Power becoming another bottleneck in the AI deployment
HVDC adoption emerging from Vera Rubin
In the power space, HVDC has been one of the key focuses in the AI cluster, as the rapid
increase in AI rack power density is creating growing challenges for conventional power-
distribution architectures. As rack-level power consumption rising from tens of kilowatts
to several hundreds of KWs, electrical losses, cable size, thermal management
requirements, and overall system complexity increase significantly. Under traditional low-

voltage power architectures, delivering larger amounts of power requires proportionally
higher current, which increases resistive losses and places greater stress on cables,
connectors, busbars, and power-conversion equipment.
At the same time, every power-conversion stage introduces efficiency losses and
generates additional heat. As AI deployments scale into multi-megawatt and eventually
gigawatt-level campuses, even small efficiency improvements can translate into

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
meaningful reductions in operating costs, cooling requirements, and infrastructure
investment. Consequently, the industry is increasingly focused on power architectures
that can reduce conversion stages, lower current levels, and improve overall power-
delivery efficiency.
Against this backdrop, high-voltage direct current (HVDC) is emerging as one of the most
important architectural upgrades for next-generation AI infrastructure. By increasing

distribution voltage, HVDC allows the same amount of power to be delivered at a lower
current, reducing conduction losses and improving system efficiency. Higher-voltage
architectures also improve scalability for ultra-high-density rack deployments by
alleviating some of the physical and thermal constraints associated with conventional
power-distribution systems.
Exhibit 18: Better power efficiency for 800VDC architecture
Source: Delta
Exhibit 19: Better efficiency leads to cost savings

Source: Delta
In a joint keynote speech by Delta and Nvidia, the roadmap illustrates the industry's
gradual migration toward higher-voltage power architectures. Earlier AI server generations
primarily utilized conventional power-delivery schemes centered around rack-level 48V
distribution. As rack power requirements increased with Blackwell-generation systems,
industry participants began evaluating higher-voltage architectures capable of supporting

substantially greater power density. Looking ahead, the industry is increasingly converging
around 800VDC as a potential next-generation standard for large-scale AI deployments,
particularly as rack-level power consumption approaches several hundred kilowatts. The
transition reflects the need to improve power-distribution efficiency while supporting the
continued growth of AI computing infrastructure.
Though we also saw the 800VDC power rack reference design for the Vera Rubin rack in a

1-to-2 configuration (one 660KW power rack supporting two Vera Rubin racks), we think
the adoption of this solution might be relatively low, since the Vera Rubin rack also has a
reference design for the in-rack power shelf design

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 11
Exhibit 20: 800VDC roadmap
Source: Delta
Exhibit 21: 800VDC reference design for VR
Source: Delta
Liquid cooled power shelf on the horizon
The next key check point to watch should be the Vera Rubin Ultra generation, as the
power consumption per rack shall continue to increase and there might be more hardware
devices to enable the inter-rack scale up network, both could take up more space on the
IT rack. That said, the power shelf will shift from air cooling into liquid cooling, which

could narrow the power shelf height by 1U to free up some space as well. Our checks
indicate that both the in-rack power shelf and 800VDC power rack solutions are ongoing
now, and we believe that rack space, power redundancy and system reliability are all the
factors being considered. We'll closely monitor developments on this front.

Source: Megmeet
Exhibit 23: Liquid cooled power shelf in rack
Source: Megmeet

In the MGX ecosystem board, where Nvidia usually list the recommenced vendor lists and
product samples, we saw SST this time. Delta, Heron. DG Matrax, Sungrow, Solaredge,
Megmeet, Luxshare, etc.

### Page 12

M
Idea
12
Exhibit 24: SST vendors on the Nvidia MGX ecosystem board
Source: Morgan Stanley
Looking further ahead, Solid-State Transformers (SSTs) represent a potential next-
generation evolution of power infrastructure. Unlike traditional transformers that operate
primarily as passive devices, SSTs leverage power electronics to provide voltage
conversion, power conditioning, and bidirectional energy management within a single
platform. This enables greater controllability and flexibility while supporting increasingly

digitalized power networks.
Although SSTs remain at an early stage of commercialization and face challenges related
to economics, reliability, and large-scale deployment, the technology aligns well with long-
term data center trends. As AI campuses evolve into utility-scale power consumers
incorporating energy storage, distributed generation, and advanced power management,
SSTs could eventually become a key enabling technology.

### Page 13

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 13
Therefore, while HVDC is expected to drive the next phase of power architecture
upgrades, SSTs represent a longer-term technology roadmap that could further transform
how future data centers distribute and manage electricity.

According to Infineon, SST could represent an emerging >$1bn market opportunity by
2030, as the technology begins to replace a portion of the >$15bn conventional
transformer market, particularly in smaller-power applications. While SST penetration is
expected to remain relatively modest versus the overall transformer market, the
opportunity is significant for relevant players in the longer term.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Lite-On Technology (2301.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 17x based on average 2027-28 EPS. We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 47% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4, versus
the stock's 16x P/E and 2% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 7.2.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger optical business growth
n Stronger data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand growth

n Downtrend in raw material prices
Risks to Downside
n Macro demand weakness
n Weaker data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand slowdown
n Uptrend in raw material prices
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.

Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth
n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 45x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of

~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for
2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Global Idea
Greater China Semiconductors
OCP Wrap-up: Rising Optics and
Al Infrastructure Alternatives
Our US trip highlighted ICA/IPO traction, complementary rack-
management silicon, hardware security and CXL-based memory
pooling, with potential storage server upside.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst
Daniel.Yen@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2863
Charlie Chan
Equity Analyst

Morgan Stanley Asia Limited+
Henry Zhao
Equity Analyst
Henry.Zhao@morganstanley.com                                                +852 2239-7731
Ethan Jia
Research Associate
Ethan.Jia@morganstanley.com                                                +852 3963-2287
Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
ICA/IPO: Gaining traction, but cost still a hurdle. A China CSP outlined optical
form factors from XPO/NPO to interposer optics (IPO) and CPO, including

integration of IPO with interposer copper attach (ICA). TE Connectivity showcased
224G/448G NPO/CPO sockets and ICA sockets with 0.4mm pitch and up to 10,000
pins, offering high-density connectivity, low crosstalk and improved reworkability.
We see rising traction for ICA/IPO, with potential mass production in 2027   (For
more, see our recent CPO report).
Marvell IMC: Complementary to AST2700, not a replacement. Marvell
demonstrated the OCTEON-based infrastructure management controller (IMC)

addresses higher-level Al rack/cluster telemetry and control; ASPEED's AST2700
BMC continues to handle node-level security and remote management. Given
distinct functions and software ecosystems, we do not view IMC as a direct
AST2700 competitor. We also don't see an AST2700 production issue, supporting
an on-track ramp. The key debate is actually market share in TPU/Axion.
Caliptra: Expanding hardware Root of Trust opportunities. The open Caliptra

framework defines silicon-level Root of trust (RoT) for data-center processors and
accelerators, supporting secure boot, device identity and attestation eMemory
(3529.TW) subsidiary PUFsecurity offers PUFrt, providing NeoPUF-derived device,
NeoFuse OTP secure storage and hardware entropy as physical security building
blocks for Caliptra implementations. Broader adoption could support incremental
PUF/security-IP licensing, although monetization depends on qualifications and

customer design wins. (For more, see our quantum security report).
Other: CXL fabric, cooling and compute ramps: The microchannel lid
demonstration underscored the importance of chip-level thermal design as rack
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
ICA/IPO traction: China CSP and TE demonstrations suggest growing adoption,
although cost could be higher
IMC versus BMC: Marvell IMC complements AST2700; we see no production
disruption.
Hardware security: Caliptra strengthens the case for eMemory PUF-based
security IP.
CXL and thermal: CXL supports memory pooling: Cooling and ASIC/CPU ramps

remain in focus. Muse is likely driving storage server demand.

of CXL fabric switches to connect multiple hosts with shared memory at rack scale,
despite still taking time for maturity. Custom ASIC and server CPU ramps remain on
track. Storage server demand could rise on Muse.

Not-Rated (NR) - Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's
industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U or Under) - The stock's total return is expected to be below the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry

team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

大立光(3008) 
買進, TP 7,800 (潛在報酬 17.12%)  
買進 
Price(10/08):6,660 
Target Price：7,800

大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響 ， 加上新產
品良率及綠電採購成本壓力 ， 預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對
保守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司
發揮精密光學設計與製造優勢；另一方面， FAU 預計於 2H27
進入量產，隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來
源。 考量大立光具備高精度光學製造、 微米級對位技術及自動化
設備開發能力， 技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢， 正面看待公
司中長期發展。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入
為 706.92 億元，Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，
營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，
Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。研究部考量儘管 2026 年消費
性電子需求疲弱，且 CPO 產品短期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度
優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，2027 年營運有望重回成長
軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目標價訂為 7,800 元，投
資建議為買進。  
⚫ 公司基本資料 
⚫ Largan 大立光電(3008.TT)公司簡介 
大立光電(3008.TT)成立於 1987 年，並於 2002 年台灣證券交易所
掛牌上市。大立光作為全球光學塑膠鏡頭領導廠商，主要業務內容
為研發與製造精密光學塑膠鏡頭，產品主要應用於智慧型手機，其
餘也包含平板、筆記型電腦、車用及其他消費性電子產品，在全球
光學鏡頭市占率約佔 30%。其總部位於台中市南屯區精密機械園
區，共有十座廠區及兩座興建中廠區。前十大股東持股合計
38.06%。客戶為 APPLE、華為、三星、小米、OPPO、VIVO 等手
機品牌廠，主要競爭者則為玉晶光 (3406.TT) 及舜宇光學
(2382.HK)。3Q26 出貨量佔比：20MP 以上約 10~20%、10MP 約

圖一、大立光 3Q26 出貨量佔比

資料來源：公司資料、第一金投顧彙整

⚫ 產業概況 
記憶體成本飆升壓抑出貨，智慧型手機市場進入量縮價增 
根據研調機構數據 ，2026 年全球智慧型手機市場受記憶體供應短缺
及成本大幅上漲影響 ， 出貨量預估將Y-16.7%至 10.5 億支 ， 較前一
季預估的 Y-13.9%再度下修，並創下歷年最大年度衰退幅度，其中
2H26 出貨量更預估 Y-27.2%。 由於記憶體成本年增超過300%，品
牌廠難以自行吸收成本，陸續透過漲價及調整產品組合因應，帶動
2026 年智慧型手機平均售價 Y+27.6%至 581 美元，全球市場產值
反而 Y+6.3%至 6,130 億美元，呈現「量縮價增」格局。產品結構
方面，低階市場受到衝擊最為明顯，尤其100 美元以下機種因原先
毛利空間有限，品牌廠陸續縮減產品線，2Q26 該價位出貨量已年
減近 60%；相較之下，高階機種因消費者價格敏感度較低，且成熟
市場具分期付款等機制支撐，需求相對具韌性，帶動整體市場加速
朝高階化發展。

⚫ 未來展望 
拉貨動能逐步趨緩，良率改善有望緩解毛利率壓力 
展望 4Q26，預期 10 月拉貨量與 9 月持平，11 月起則隨新機拉貨
高峰結束而逐步下滑 ， 儘管客戶1H27 新產品已自 10 月開始拉貨，
仍難以完全彌補既有訂單下修影響，加上目前客戶需求能見度偏
短，第四季營收動能仍具不確定性。毛利率方面，儘管新機生產良
率逐步改善 ， 然而綠電採購成本持續增加 ， 相關成本最快須至2028
年才能反映於報價，預期短期毛利率改善幅度仍將受到限制。綜合
以上 ， 研究部預估大立光4Q26 營業收入為 155.21 億元 ，Y-9.86%、
Q-0.83%，毛利率 46.65%，Y-1.32ppts、Q+4.44ppts，營業利益
率 35.29%，Y-1.46ppts、Q+5.04ppts，稅後純益 54.91 億元，
Y-18.28%，EPS 為 41.98 元。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2026
年營業收入為 603.80 億元 ，Y-1.26%， 毛利率46.84%，Y-3.59ppts，
營 業 利 益 率 35.06% ，Y-3.47ppts ， 稅 後 純 益 214.67 億元，
Y+0.90%，EPS 為 164.12 元。

手機主鏡頭規格持續升級，高階光學產品支撐營運動能 
展望 2027 年，智慧型手機主鏡頭將有規格升級，帶動高階鏡頭設
計提升，目前相關產品已完成技術準備並持續送樣，有望隨新機導
入逐步貢獻營收。潛望式鏡頭方面，下一波較大幅度規格升級預計
落在 2028 年，目前已進入試產階段，2027 年則以既有產品出貨為
主。獲利方面，因綠電採購成本持續增加，短期毛利率改善仍將取
決於新產品良率及產品組合變化。

FA 預計 2H27 量產，精密對位與整合能力開啟光通訊新成長動能 
光通方面，FA 產品已通過客戶認證，FAU 則持續進行送樣及整合
驗證 ，FAU 預計 2027 年年中完成產線建置 ，2H27 正式進入量產。
初期以單排 FA 及 Grating Couplin 技術為主，並朝 FAU 與 Carrier
整合出貨發展 ， 提高單一產品附加價值 ， 多排FA 及 Edge Coupling
則持續開發 ， 預計2028 年後逐步導入 。 公司憑藉微米級精密對位、
自製自動化設備及檢測能力 ， 可望在高技術門檻的FAU 市場建立競
爭優勢。預期 2027 年光通營收貢獻仍處於起步階段，隨後續產能
與良率提升，有望成為中長期成長動能。綜合以上，研究部預估大
立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元 ，Y+17.08%， 毛利率51.37%，
Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24
億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。

⚫ 結論與建議 
大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響，加上新產品
良率及綠電採購成本壓力，預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對保
守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司發揮
精密光學設計與製造優勢 ； 另一方面 ，FAU 預計於 2H27 進入量產 ，
隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來源。考量大立
光具備高精度光學製造、微米級對位技術及自動化設備開發能力，
技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢，正面看待公司中長期發展。綜
合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元，
Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，
Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08
元。研究部考量儘管2026 年消費性電子需求疲弱 ，且CPO 產品短
期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，
2027 年營運有望重回成長軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目
標價訂為 7,800 元，投資建議為買進。

jasperyeh@yuanta.com

估 3Q26 稅後 12 億元，季增 15%，年增 27%，EPS 7.67 元 
3Q26進入旺季，營收季增 3%至 496億元，年增 48%，估毛利率季減 0.1
百分點至 19.2%，主因低毛利率子公司帆宣比重提高 0.3百分點及記憶體持
續上漲。估稅後 12億元，季增 15%，年增 27%，EPS 7.6 7元，比原估下
修 5%，主因營收低於原先預期 4%。4Q26 NCR代工訂單/帆宣美國二廠持
續增加，估營收季增 3%，毛利率季持平，稅後 14.3 億元，季增 19%，
EPS 9.1元，比原估上修 8%，主因 Kontron持股由 30%上升至 49%。 
提高 Kontron 持股，有助中長期投資效益 
樺漢提高德國子公司 Kontron AG 持股由原先 29.7%上升至 49.4%(8/20交
割完成 )。影響： 1)財務面：本中心 預期提高持股後， 估計可望提升獲利
10%以上。2)軟硬體與通訊互補：Kontron具有嵌入式運算、5G通訊、資安、
交通及國防航太技術，結合樺漢在邊緣 AI 伺服器、機器人及強固型工業電
腦的硬體優勢，從「單點設備銷售」轉型為「平台化解決方案」。 
上修 2026/27 年獲利 1%/6% 
智慧工廠與廠務(帆宣)目前在手訂單 1,578 億元，MoM+17%，未來成長趨
勢明確。展望 2026~27 年主要工程認列包括台積美國 P2~P3及 AP、高雄
廠 P3~P4、新竹 20廠 P3、台中 18廠 P9等，看好新廠商機，包括設備代
工、代理等，其中封裝設備估計 >3 成之成長。由於認列美國二廠業績加快，
估 2026/27年營收年增 55%/14%至 801/913億元，創新高。 
合計樺漢 2026/27 年營收 1,864/2,104 億元，成長 31%/13%，規模經濟向
上，費用率下滑，估營業利益率 5.57%/5.64%，優於 2025年 3.99%。稅後
44.4/53.6 億元，年增 38%/21%，EPS 2 8.25/34.14 元，比先前預期上修
1%/6%，主因升高子公司 Kontron持股。樺漢受惠提升 Kontron持股及帆宣
認列美國二廠增加，2026/27年營收及獲利高成長，維持買進評等。 
元大觀點 
⧫

記憶體漲價大部分轉嫁給客戶，略影響毛利率，但規模經濟向上，營業利益率上揚 
關於記憶體漲價因應，公司已與供應商鎖定全年約 200億元訂單所需之記憶體供貨量，價格雖隨市
場浮動，但會透過長約定價或調價等轉嫁給客戶，若記憶體價格漲幅過大也採客戶 consign 模式，
降低毛利率影響。1Q26 部分客戶接受轉嫁，部分客戶則選擇先觀望遞延專案，造成營收不如預期。
但 2Q26 後客戶已逐漸接受記憶體漲價，出貨恢復正常。由於記憶體轉嫁有落後且難以全數移轉，
加上近期低毛利率的子公司帆宣廠務成長較快，因此估 2026/27 年毛利率 19.4%/19.3%，略低於
2025年 20.5%。但預估 2026/27年整體營收成長 31%/13%，規模經濟明顯向上，費用率下滑，營
業利益率 5.57%/5.64%，遠優於 2025年 3.99%。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：工業電腦 2026年 10月 19日 Ennoconn 第4 頁，共 19頁  
積極佈局實體 AI 
公司將實體 AI(Physical AI)定義為 AI由數位分析與內容生成，進一步具備感知、理解、決策及實際
執行能力。樺漢過去由 IPC 1.0 工業電腦、IPC 2.0 工業物聯網及 IPC 3.0 智慧平台逐步升級，
2026年開始進一步布局 Physical AI。 
公司建立完整機器人系統工程團隊，涵蓋 AI 感知與理解、機器學習系統、機器人運動控制、嵌入
式運算平台、數位孿生模擬及可靠度設計。 AI 感知包含影像、語音、感測器融合及 VLA（Vision-
Language-Action）技術；模擬驗證採用 NVIDIA Isaac Sim 進行虛擬場景、產線及機器人流程模擬
測試，用以最佳化生產效率。樺漢競爭策略由單純提供板卡與運算模組，轉向提供完整機器人系統
平台。產品布局涵蓋 Sense、Compute、Connect、Control及 Act五大機器人平台零件，應用包括
AMR自主移動機器人、人形機器人、工業製造、智慧物流及商業服務。  
運動控制及機器人相關技術仍在持續投資；模擬驗證軟體毛利率可超過 60%。管理層認為 Physical 
AI 初期仍以研發與平台建置為主，短期商業化重點為工業及商用 AI 應用，人形機器人獲利貢獻仍
需時間。2026年實體 AI為初期發展階段，貢獻樺漢業績約 3億美金，占營收比重 5.2%。長期實
體 AI 於製造場域可協助採購與物流管理、改善長短料問題，並透過即時感測與自動化控制提升工
站品質、精度及生產效率，研調機構預估全球實體 AI產值由 2025年 58億美金，上升至 2035元
產值可達 1200 億美元， CAGR 35.4%。樺漢結合既有智慧工廠與工業物聯網客戶基礎，推動
Physical AI走向實際部署，可望快速成長。

圖 8：樺漢機器人與智慧餐飲佈局

資料來源：公司資料、元大投顧整理 
提高 Kontron 持股，有助中長期投資效益 
因樺漢集團對 Kontron的總持股比例已來到約 29.72%，接近德國證券法規定的 30%強制公開收購
門檻，樺漢董事會正式授權董事長啟動現金公開收購之前置作業(訂定每股 23.5歐元)。樺漢公開收
購並提高德國子公司 Kontron AG 持股由原先 29.7%上升至 49.4%(8/20交割完成)，核心效益與影
響主要體現在以下幾個面向： 
1)財務面：本中心預期提高持股後，估計可望提升獲利 10%以上(少數股權項目扣除減少)，雙方深
化整合後，透過樺漢母公司鴻海製造資源，預期從 2027 年開始全面發酵具體的營收與成本規模經
濟效益。

### Page 6

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：工業電腦 2026年 10月 19日 Ennoconn 第6 頁，共 19頁  
2)業務與技術整合(實體 AI 平台化) 
• 軟硬體與通訊互補：Kontron具有嵌入式運算、5G通訊、資安、交通及國防航太技術，結合樺漢
在邊緣 AI 伺服器、機器人及強固型工業電腦的硬體優勢，從「單點設備銷售」轉型為「平台化解
決方案」。 
• 加速跨場景落地：有助於將無人結帳、智慧零售、 AMR（自主移動機器人）及工廠自動化等應用
標準化，降低客戶導入門檻，並加快在北美及歐洲市場的開案與交付效率。  
Kontron 成長領域包括交通運輸、國防航太、 5G/FRMCS/NAD，以及軟體與解決方案，營收目標
由 2026年 16億歐元上升至 2030年 26億歐元(CAGR 13%)、EBITDA達 4.2億歐元，並藉由提高
平台及服務收入占比改善獲利結構。

結盟 NCR 增加 ODM 訂單 2Q26 開始出貨，有助工業物聯網部門成長 
樺漢與 NCR Voyix (股票代號：VYX US)結盟，該公司是全球消費者技術的領導者，主要生產 POS、
自動櫃員機、條碼閱讀器等，提供高品質的技術解決方案，並幫助企業在快速變化的數位環境中 蓬
勃發展。NCR Voyix 在市場領先的軟體和服務與樺漢的硬體專業知識技術高度互補，並有助樺漢
ESaaS及 ODM業務進一步擴展，為雙贏的結盟。 
NCR年營收約 1,230億元，其中硬體占比 32%，軟體 68%，硬體部分約 400億元台幣訂單未來全
交給樺漢代工，其中 160億元為新接訂單[NCR之前即為樺漢 ODM客戶，60%硬體(約 240億元)
交樺漢生產]，此部分將貢獻在工業物聯網 (含 Kontron、ODM)業務上，2025 年因應川普關稅議題，
NCR IT 全球性服務需調整期，發展整合系統；樺漢墨西哥製造基地調整至田納西。因此原本增加
NCR代工訂單由 10月再度延至 2026年 1月出貨，後因美伊戰爭及 DRAM漲價影響，客戶延至
2Q26正式出貨。受惠此訂單增加，估 2026/27 年工業物聯網營收成長 24%/13%至 845/958億元。

智慧工廠與廠務(帆宣)進入美國二廠密集認列期，營運創新高 
智慧工廠與廠務(帆宣)目前在手訂單 1,578億元，MoM+17%，主要來自晶圓代工廠、記憶體廠擴
廠積極，另外新增資料中心建置，半導體供應鏈普遍面臨交期、產能壓力，加上具有能力承接大型
專案廠商有限，使得廠務接近賣方市場，未來 2~3 年成長趨勢更為明確。展望 2026~27 年主要
工程認列包括台積美國 P2~P3及 AP、高雄廠 P3~P4、新竹 20廠 P3、台中 18廠 P9等，並且看
好新廠商機，包括設備代工、代理等，其中封裝設備估計 >3 成之成長。智慧工廠與廠務(帆宣) 認
列美國二廠業績加快，2026 年前 9 月營收成長 56%至 574 億元，第四季旺季營收持續向上，估
2026/27年營收年增 55%/14%至 801/913億元，創新高。 
2026/27 年年營收及獲利維持高成長 
智慧軟體方案策略進入 IPC 3.0Ⅱ階段，推廣工控/智慧平台，未來專注於雲端+邊緣 AI與大數據外，
亦提升工業級語言模型與機器人技術，聚焦產業包括：國防、航太、交通、零售、建築等。產品包
括：Edge AI Server & Device、Token、AI Agent、Physical AI等，公司積極佈局中，近期因專案
認列延後，估 2026 年營收 218 億元，衰退 3%。2027 年代理/實體 AI 發酵，估營收成長 7%至
233億元。 
合計樺漢 2026/27年營收 1,864/2,104億元(下修 2%/2%，主因智慧軟體方案營收低於預期)，成長
31%/13%，低毛利率的智慧工廠與廠務(帆宣)成長較大及上游原物料 Dram大漲，估 2026/27年毛

產業概況  
工業電腦未來 CAGR 6%，為下游成長穩定的產業 
工業電腦在 2000 年後網路發達，亦造成工業電腦用途日漸擴大，由工廠發展至生活端。過去常受
全球製造業景氣影響，後因應用面變廣，成長性加快。 
工業電腦近年成長率在 5~10%，是下游成長穩定的產業。根據研調機構統計及預估，工業電腦
2023年產值約為 45億美元，可望增加至 2037年的 96億美元，CAGR 6%比一般 PC低個位數成
長為佳，主因公司對工業物聯網、製造業數位化、邊緣 AI 建置…等因素推動，其中半導體、製造
業、汽車、物流、零售…等均為重要成長來源。 
圖 9：工業電腦 2023-2037 年產值 CAGR 6%

資料來源：Markets and Markets、元大投顧整理 
工業 4.0 帶動物聯網需求大增 
從 18世紀末經歷多次工業生產變革，20世紀末進入 21世紀，工業製造由 3.0發展至 4.0，其中最
大改變就是組裝、加工、搬運 …等由原先自動化機台 (工業 3.0)，加入智能回饋，透過物聯網收集
巨量資料，傳輸至雲端運算，統計分析後，即時改善生產製程。隨時因應機台老舊、材料或周遭環
境變化，自動調整量產系統。 
第四次工業革命和過去相比，網路無所不在，移動性提高；感測器體積變得更小、性能更強大、成
本也更低。與此同時，人工智慧和機器學習也開始嶄露鋒芒，相關應用不斷增加。

### Page 9

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：工業電腦 2026年 10月 19日 Ennoconn 第9 頁，共 19頁  
圖 10：歷次工業變革

資料來源：元大投顧整理 
預估人工智慧物聯網(AIoT)2025~2034 年 CAGR 達 22.16% 
物聯網基礎架構分為五層：物件、通訊與網路、運算與儲存、應用與服務、分析與統計。台灣工業
電腦公司較專注在前端物件 (感測元件)以及中端的運算與儲存二項業務。尤其前端的感測元件為台
灣強項，諸如：POS 機、ATM、機器人手臂、安控攝影機…等。近年受惠人工智慧(AI)導入，成長
加快。 
全球人工智慧物聯網(AIoT)未來受惠於 Data Center、AI Server 建置、企業數位轉型…等因素穩定
成長，研調機構預估 AIoT 2025~2034 年 CAGR 22.16%，為未來工業電腦成長重心。人工智慧物
聯網(AIoT)在全球使用廣泛，目前工業電腦產業使用方面已經陸續切入智慧家庭、車聯網、自動化
設備、機器人…等領域。

線亦提升生產效率，以福特汽
車為代表。

效率、品質。

網、巨量資料、雲端服務，對

控制。

獲利調整與股票評價
上修 2026/27 年稅後 1%/6%
下修樺漢 2026/27 年營收 2%/2%至 1,864/2104 億元，主因智慧軟體方案營收低於預期，成長
31%/13%，規模經濟明顯向上，費用率下滑，營業利益率 5.57%/5.64%，遠優於 2025 年 3.99%。
合計稅後 44.4/53.6億元，年增 38%/21%，EPS 2 8.25/34.14元(以最新稀釋後股本 15.7億元計算)，
比先前預期上修 1%/6%，主因提高 Kontron持股，少數股權項目扣除降低。

維持買進評等，目標價 545 元，潛在上漲空間 34.7%
與樺漢同樣從事工業電腦相關，在國內主要為研華、神基、飛捷、廣積、振樺電等。以 2027 年預

ODM 訂單開始出貨，加上子公司帆宣認列美國二廠進入高峰期，營收快速成長。另外樺漢增加
Kontron 持股，不僅提升獲利，亦有助長期代理/實體 AI 發展，因此維持買進評等。目標本益比同
先前 16倍，以 2027年 EPS 34.14元計算，目標價 545元，潛在上漲空間 34.7%。

公司簡介  
樺漢專精於工業電腦硬體解決方案。終端應用包括 POS機、銀行自動化、互動式資訊服務系統、彩券機及工業自動化。產
品組合方面(2025)，工業物聯網 48%、智慧工廠與廠務 36%、智慧軟體與方案 16%。銷售區域 (2025)北美 26%、歐洲
37%、中國 9%、其他 28%。主要生產交由母公司鴻海代工，另外樺漢在歐洲新設匈牙利自有工廠，取得當地娛樂、金融
零售、工業網安等訂單，維繫 ODM業務成長。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：工業電腦 2026年 10月 19日 Ennoconn 第16 頁，共 19頁

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 樺漢整體的 ESG風險評級屬於低風險，於 Sustainalytics資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於領先的位置，
在科技硬體行業中的公司中排名略為領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 樺漢的整體曝險屬於中等水準，略高於科技硬體行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的  ESG 議
題包含公司資安、商業道德、人力資源等。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：樺漢已採取若干措施來管理與重大 ESG議題相關的風險，但這些措施尚不足以涵蓋
其所面臨的所有風險。儘管如此，該公司並未捲入具有重大財務影響的風險事件。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

## Document 274: EcoNews of 12 October 2026 (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 12 October 2026 (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Stronger trade posture pays off. The EU and China have agreed to
restrain hybrid cars exports to the EU to a level half or less than cur -
rently projected over the next four years, cutting exports by “several
million units” though still allowing them to rise. China also agreed to

drop its tariffs on EUR 4 bn of EU exports and provide easier access to
Chinese rare earths and magnets under a fast-track licensing sche -
me. These announcements were made at the conclusion of EU Trade
Commissioner Maroš Šefcovic’s visit to Beijing, following a recent es -
calation in trade tensions. Meanwhile, France and Germany released
a joint non-paper on Strengthening European resilience, addressing
global macroeconomic imbalances and market distorting practices

calling notably for:
i) Broadening the tools the European Commission may use to address
unfair trade practices (including undervalued exchange rates) or im -
port surges;
ii) Procedural changes to make them easier to use (qualified majority
of member states needed to block rather than authorise them). These
measures will be discussed during the next EU council on October
15-16th.
The European Commission also unveiled a new critical materials pac -

kage involving 46 strategic extraction, processing or recycling projects
spread across 16 member states (including 5 in France). The goal is to
reduce the EU’s dependence on China for critical metals.
EUROZONE
Price pressures are broadening, activity holds up. Producer price in -
flation accelerated further in August (8.3% y/y 1 from 5.8%), driven by
energy. Pressures are becoming more widespread, notably in capital
goods and above all intermediate goods (6.8%). They are partly offset

by non-durable consumer goods, which remain in deflation (-0.8%).
Given the PPI's close correlation with the HICP , this points to further
upside risks to consumer inflation. On the activity front, final September
PMIs confirmed the flash estimates, with the composite index still solid
at 53.1 and the manufacturing index stable at 52.7. The construction
PMI improved but remains depressed (43.4; +0.4 pt). Retail sales edged
up 0.1% m/m2 in August.

France
Modest spread relief as budget positioning starts. The spread between
French and German 10Y yields narrowed to 136 bps 3 on October 9
(mostly thanks to a decline in the French yield). As the Finance Com -
mittee of the National Assembly started discussing the 2027 budget,
the government reiterated its strong intent not to deviate from the
5% of GDP deficit target and to get a budget adopted in time, likely
without a vote. This is the most likely outcome — but not to be im -

plemented before 10 December (given mandatory timelines) — which
effectively removes any incentive for MPs to back unpopular measures
during the process. We can therefore expect heated debates (including
multiple no-confidence votes being tabled but rejected) and a lack in
fiscal discipline. In parallel, the "Rassemblement National" party ("Na-
tional Rally" in English) presented its fiscal strategy, which includes an
alternative budget for 2027 and an aggressive fiscal consolidation path

throughout 2032. The party called for a referendum to constitutionalise
1 Year-on-year
2 Month-on-month
3 Basis points
a “golden rule” that would require a deficit at least 0.5 pp below the
debt-stabilising threshold (roughly 3% of GDP), as long as the public
debt ratio exceeds 60% of GDP . The golden rule would require a budget
balance adjustment of 0.5 pp per year if the fiscal deficit is more than
1 pp above the stabilising threshold. The adjustment measures put

forward lack specifics but likely overstate savings, understate costs
and rely on overly optimistic growth assumptions (despite large fiscal
consolidation, at least 1.7% annually over the next three years — a
pace reached only 4 times in France since 2008, apart from post-pan-
demic reopening, and well in excess of the roughly 1% growth potential
estimated by most international institutions). That said, it is significant
that this party has chosen to fly the flag for fiscal discipline loud and

clear. Other parties should do the same.
Activity is holding up. New vehicle registrations rose 11% y/y in Sep -
tember, confirming a marked acceleration over the past three months
(+9.4% y/y in Q3). In Q3, growth came entirely from electric vehicles
(+112% y/y), whose share of registrations reached an all-time high
(38% in Q3, 41% in September). Export growth continued in August,
both in services (+5.2% y/y YTD) and in goods (+5.6% y/y YTD) — though

driven by prices rather than volume in the latter case. Manufacturing
output posted only a moderate rebound in August (+0.3% m/m; fol -
lowing cumulative declines of 2.8% over the previous three months),
limited by renewed supply constraints in Q3 (which limited produc -
tion at 31.5% of companies; +5 pp compared to H1). Industrial produc-
tion fell (-0.3% m/m) as a result of a decline in electricity production
(-2.4% m/m) driven by the heatwave. Credit growth remains dynamic.

Bank and market financing to NFCs saw steady growth in August (+4%).
New housing loans rebounded slightly in August (EUR 12.2 bn) fol -
lowing a relatively weak July, whilst interest rates continued to rise.
Consumer credit remained buoyant.
Upcoming: Banque de France monthly Business Survey (Monday), Bu-
siness creations in September (Friday).
Germany
Bullish official growth forecast upgrade despite mixed data. The
German government raised its 2026 GDP growth forecast to 1.3%

— the fastest pace since 2017 — from a prior estimate of 0.5%, citing
stronger-than-expected exports and a surge in government spending.
The 2027 forecast was also raised to 1.1% from 0.9%. Industrial pro -
duction was stronger than expected in August (+2.3% y/y versus -1.7%
in July; the highest level since March 2025 and higher than antici -
pated), driven mainly by strong growth in construction output, prima -
rily in public works (+10.5%; to the highest level since November 2021).

By contrast, manufacturing output fell by -0.2% (after +3.3% in July)
due to a decline in transport equipment production (lowest level since
April 2022). Likewise, new factory orders hit their lowest level in one
year in August (-10.6% m/m). However, this decline is mainly due
to a base effect. Export growth remains solid: +4.4% YTD, despite a
-0.8% m/m decline in August. Exports to the Eurozone continue to rise
(+6.3% y/y YTD), while exports to the U.S. have returned to positive

territory (+1.1%) and those to China continue to decline (-12.9%). The
trade surplus reached EUR 19.5 bn in August (+4% y/y YTD, despite a
-9.8% m/m decline).

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026

ECONEWS Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
UNITED STATES
Mild slowdown in services and more rate hikes signaled. The non-ma-
nufacturing ISM survey fell 0.5 pp4 to 54.9 in September due to a sharp
slowdown in the business activity component (down 5.2 pp to 56.5 pp,
still a robust expansion). Employment rose and supply pressures in -
tensified for the first time since June, while the price gauge reached

its highest level (74, up 1.4 pp) since 2022. Meanwhile, the minutes
of the September FOMC meeting confirmed the Fed’s hawkish outlook
and noted that the AI-buildout carried growing weight in the reading of
current inflation data and outlook. Governor Waller and St. Louis Fed
President Musalem mentioned the need for additional rate hikes while
remaining flexible on timing. The New York Fed survey of consumer in-
flation expectations showed them rising to 3.9%. We continue to expect

two more hikes, the next one coming in December.
Upcoming: September CPI (Wednesday), September PPI and Retail
Sales (Thursday), September Industrial Production (Friday).
JAPAN
Solid wage growth fails to lift household spending. Total wage growth
eased to 3.8% y/y in August (down 0.5 pp) as bonus payments stalled
(0% y/y, after 5.3% y/y in July). This allowed continued real wage growth
at 1.5% y/y, extending an uninterrupted run of positive readings in 2026

as administrative measures kept inflation in check. However, this did
not prevent real household spending from contracting in August for the
ninth consecutive month (-3.1% y/y).
EMERGING MARKETS
The IIF reports that nonresident portfolio flows to EMs turned negative
at -USD 26.3 bn in September, after USD 11.4 bn in August, the first net
outflow since June, as a hawkish Fed lifted US real yields and the USD.
Equity outflows reached -USD 19.2 bn, led by heavy foreign selling of

Korean equities, while debt flows turned negative at -USD 7.0 bn as
hard currency demand faded and EM credit spreads widened late in
the month. Further hikes from DM central banks will create additional
headwinds, but EM should remain resilient as long as global growth
persists.
ASIA
China
Yuan policy defence. Coinciding with the Beijing visit of the EU trade
commissioner, the People's Bank of China issued an unusually lengthy
formal statement rejecting claims that the yuan is undervalued and

defending its exchange-rate management by reiterating that it has a
‘floating exchange rate regime’ with reference to a basket of currencies.
It further argued that China's trade surpluses are driven by rising in -
dustrial competitiveness rather than currency under-devaluation and
that exchange-rate adjustments will not resolve structural global im -
balances. Nonetheless, it allowed the yuan to hit a four-year high vs.
the CFETS basket — bringing its year-to-date gain to 5.7%. The PBOC

set the USD/CNY's reference rate at 6.7330, the strongest level since
2023.
India
Monetary tightening. The RBI raised its repo rate by 25bp to 5.5%, its
first hike since February 2023, and shifted to a “calibrated tightening”
stance amidst rising inflationary pressures. The RBI introduced new
measures to stabilise the rupee, including direct dollar supplies to
state-owned oil companies and tighter restrictions on FX derivatives.
4 Percentage points

EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST, AFRICA
Central Europe
Cautious monetary policy stance amid mild inflation uptick. Poland’s
and Romania’s Central banks kept policy rates unchanged at 3.75% and
6.5%, respectively. Inflation accelerated in September, with a mild up-
tick in the Czech Republic and Hungary (respectively 2.5% and 1.6% y/y)
and a more entrenched increase in Poland (4% y/y). Inflation is now
above the official target in the Czech Rep. and Poland, but fuel price

caps in both countries should contain inflation dynamics.
Gulf
PMIs for September point for a firm but uneven non-oil recovery. Both
Saudi and UAE PMIs stood at 55.3, driven mostly by export orders in
the UAE and by domestic demand in Saudi Arabia. On the contrary,
Qatar’s PMI fell again (to 46.7), while Kuwait’s PMI also declined but
remained in expansionary zone (at 52.4).
Türkiye
Manufacturing production contracted by 1.6% m/m in August, the lar-

gest monthly decrease since the beginning of the year. On a y/y ba -
sis, it fell by 2.3%. Both domestic demand and exports seem to have
contributed negatively to this poor outcome. On a positive note, in
September, CPI inflation (1.9% m/m, 27.9% y/y) slowed more than the
market expected, and exports rebounded.
LATIN AMERICA
Inflationary pressures slightly up across the region. In Brazil, headline
CPI inflation accelerated to 4.6% y/y from 4.2% y/y in August (above the

central bank's 4.5% target), partly reflecting the reversal of a tempora-
ry electricity price discount in August. In Mexico, inflation rose to 3.5%
in September from 3.3% in August, while in Colombia it reached 6.3%,
slightly up from 6.2% in August. Inflation remained high in Chile but
was stable at 4.1%. In Peru, it was also slightly up (4.2% vs. 4.1%); the
central bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 4.25% for the thirteenth
consecutive month but maintained an upward bias amid rising inflation

and energy prices.
COMMODITIES
Energy: Limited relief. Continuation of skirmishes in the Gulf of Hormuz
and escalation of the conflict between the Houthis and a Saudi-led
coalition left oil prices fluctuating around $100/b for Brent. However,
the complex security situation has been driving the cost of shipping to
record high levels and the price of physical barrel to early April levels.
Late on Friday, President Trump announced an agreement with Russian

President Putin to release to global markets 4.8 million tonnes of die -
sel (roughly 8 days of European consumption), provided conditions in
refineries allow for it, of which 300,000 tonnes immediately. The White
House also announced a suspension of sanctions on Russia’s exports
of diesel until April 2027. Energy experts expect a de minimis price
impact.
European gas prices (TTF) rose by 11.5% over the week to 82 €/MWh.
EU gas inventories reached 73.6% of full capacities on October 11 th

(the official target is 80% at end October).
Food inflation rose sharply in September. The FAO’s Food Price Index
grew by 5.8% y/y, up from just 3% in August. This was driven by cereal
(+17% y/y) and sugar prices (+15%).

## Document 275: EcoNews of 19 October 2026 (2026-10-19)

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
IMF ANNUAL MEETINGS
Resilience, but no relief. The IMF's latest global growth forecast is un-
changed from a year ago and from April 2026 (at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4%
in 2027), despite the energy shock, however with a different geogra -
phical breakdown: many energy importers and war-exposed countries
downgraded, and AI-exposed countries upgraded. Resilience was the

buzzword of the meetings, however it appeared mostly in the rear -
view mirror. Looking ahead, buffers are running thin, fiscal policy needs
urgent consolidation, and downside risks (from energy and AI exube -
rance) loom large. Global headline inflation is expected to rise to 4.7%
in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before easing back to 4.1% in
2027 (revised +0.2pp). Growth in advanced economies is revised up to
2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany), staying at 1.8% in 2027. Eu-

rozone growth is upgraded to 1.1% in 2026 (+0.2pp) and 1.2% in 2027.
US growth stays projected at 2.3% in 2026 and 2027 (+0.1pp). Germany
sees the largest G7 upgrade, to 1.3% in 2026 (+0.6pp) and 1.1% in 2027
(+0.1pp). France is the only major advanced economy downgraded, to
0.4% in 2026 (-0.2pp) and 0.7% in 2027 (-0.2pp). For more detailed
takeaways see Substack and this week’s EcoWeek editorial.
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Trade policy, competitiveness and budget agreements by year-end.

EU leaders asked the Commission to propose by year-end an instru -
ment allowing, in extreme cases, full closure of the single market to
a rival deemed to be attacking the EU economy. It is designed as a
“second-strike” deterrent against unfair practices and retaliation.
The Commission is also exploring extending the hybrid-vehicle safe -
guards approach agreed with China last week to other sectors such
as chemicals and machinery. On the "Made in Europe" label, vehicles

would need to be assembled in Europe with at least 70% of compo -
nent value (excluding batteries) sourced locally and with substantial
R&D in the EU. The UK would be included, provided London aligns with
EU trade defence measures. EU Council also called for decisive pro -
gress on the competitiveness agenda, with the single-market (inclu -
ding 26th regime) and simplification files to be wrapped up by year-end.
On the multi-annual financial framework, the objective remains a deal

by end-2026, so that legislation can be adopted in 2027.
EURO AREA
Moderate tightening does it. Multiple ECB Governing Council members,
speaking from the IMF Meetings in Bangkok, re-underwrote their
“moderate response” approach to the ongoing energy-driven infla -
tion surge. Accordingly, they signaled that additional rate action was
likely but not imminent, acknowledging that the rise in bond yields
was doing part of the job, and that there were no signs yet of second

round effects. Strengthening the case for moderation, the Eurozone in-
dustrial production fell in August for the second month running, by
0.5% m/m, pulled down by pharmaceutical output and motor vehicles.
That said, production in Netherlands surged to a new high (2.5% m/m
and 8.7% y/y), driven by AI machinery and equipment.
France
Business starts booming, insolvencies are stable, growth outlook is
downgraded. Business creations rose by 16% y/y in the 3 rd quarter

(+23% y/y in September), driven mainly by the digital sector and retail.
In parallel, business bankruptcies stabilised in the 3rd quarter according
to Altares, with a 0.5% increase y/y (compared with a 5.4% increase y/y
in Q2), and their impact on employment declined: 50,510 jobs at risk
in Q3, down 3% y/y (see our recent analysis). The Banque de France
halved its estimate for Q3 GDP to 0.1% q/q (same as Insee) due to
the manufacturing sector. Its monthly survey showed a sharp jump in

business uncertainty in September, driven by fuel prices and economic
deterioration (the most acute readings in industry and services since
April). The OAT-bund spread was rangebound (137-142).
Germany
Producer prices are rising. Wholesale prices rose 8.7% y/y (vs. 6.8% in
August) due to higher wholesale prices for energy products and inter -
mediate goods, particularly metals.
UNITED KINGDOM
Summer outperformance and kudos from IMF. Monthly GDP defied

expectations in August and posted a 0.1% m/m against a -0.1% consen-
sus. This marked a slowdown from July (0.4% m/m), but also a fourth
consecutive month of expansion. Industrial production underpinned
the advance: it accelerated to 0.8% m/m (consensus: -0.1%, prior: 0.3%)
thanks to pharmaceuticals. The IMF sees growth accelerating to 1.3%
in 2026 (+0.3 pp since July) and 1.2% in 2027. It also singled out the UK
among advanced economies for its efforts and progress in putting its

fiscal house in order.
UNITED STATES
Inflation heats up, manufacturing accelerates, but an October hike
is unlikely. CPI inflation exceeded expectations at 0.65% m/m in Sep -
tember (up 0.2pp), the fastest monthly pace since April. Accordingly,
the y/y rate climbed to 3.7% (up 0.3pp). The biggest upside surprise,
however, came from the core CPI: at 0.33% m/m (consensus: 0.2%, prior:
0.3%) and 2.6% y/y (consensus: 2.5%, prior: 2.4%); as services remained

sticky while goods accelerated to 0.3% m/m on autos and AI-related
items. Producer prices too rose well above expectations to 1.3% m/m
(consensus: 0.6%, prior: 0.5%) – the largest monthly increase since
March 2022 – and 6.3% y/y (consensus: 5.3%, prior: 5.6%). Industrial
production accelerated in September to 0.7% m/m (up 0.6pp) thanks
to utilities. Manufacturing returned to growth (0.3% m/m, up 0.5pp)
with gains broadening beyond the AI-related and aerospace sectors.

Retail sales excl. autos and gasoline, by contrast, slowed sharply to
0.2% m/m (down 0.9pp). During his speech at the IMF Meetings after
the hotter than expected inflation prints, Chair Warsh declined to hint
at an imminent rate hike. We continue to expect the next rate hike to
occur in December.
To watch this week:
- Developed markets Flash PMIs for October (Friday).
- Eurozone Germany September PPI, October ZEW (Tuesday), France Insee Business Climate (Thursday).

- UK August Employment data (Tuesday), September CPI and PPI (Wednesday), September Retail Sales (Friday).
- Japan September CPI, October PMI (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
JAPAN
Producer price inflation eases. Producer prices fell for a second month
in a row in September, down a further 0.5% m/m, while the yearly rate
slowed for the first time since February (-0.8pp), though it remains
firm at 6.8%. The IMF forecasts growth at 0.7% in both 2026 and 2027,
broadly unchanged. The yen weakened past 158 per dollar again after

reports that the BoJ is leaning against a back-to-back October rate hike.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Emerging markets
Asia’s outlook upgraded, other regions downgraded.
The IMF upgraded its Asia growth forecast to 4.8% in 2026 and 4.4% in
2027. The largest upgrades are for economies well integrated into the
AI supply chain, notably Taiwan (+5.5pp to 10.7%), South Korea (+0.7pp
to 3.3%), Singapore, Vietnam, Malaysia and to some extent Thailand.
India is forecast to grow 7% in both 2026 and 2027 (fiscal year basis),

up 0.6 and 0.3pp from July on robust domestic demand.
Central Europe’s GDP growth is projected at 2.1% in 2026, slightly
lower than April’s forecast. In 2027, it remains unchanged at 2.3%.
Growth paths are diverse within the region.
In the Middle East & North Africa, GDP is expected to contract by 2%
in 2026. GCC real GDP should fall by 4.8% with only Oman expected
to post positive growth (4.1%). A marked rebound is expected for 2027
(MENA: 7%; GCC: 9.1%).

The growth outlook for Latin America remains subdued, revised down
to 2.3% in 2026 (from 2.4%) and 2.5% in 2027 (from 2.7%). However, in
its "International Trade Outlook 2026" published on 15 October, the
Economic Commission for Latin America and the Caribbean (ECLAC) ex-
pects the region to experience a trade boom in 2026: goods exports are
projected to surge by 20% in value terms (11% from price increases and
9% from volume growth), with trade to Asia (excluding China) soaring

by 32%, while intraregional trade growth lags behind at 8%. Yet, these
export gains are concentrated in commodities and low-value-added
manufacturing, limiting their spillover to broader growth. Stronger
trade is thus providing only a modest boost to the economy.
China
Explosive export growth, subdued inflation and credit growth. In Sep-
tember, export value growth surged to 36.3% y/y from 25% in August,
driven by semiconductors, computer equipment and other AI goods, as

well as green technologies. Export prices surged, especially for semi -
conductors, while total export volumes remained robust. Import value
growth also accelerated to 31% y/y from 28.2% in August, boosted by
both rising volume and rapidly increasing prices. The trade surplus
hit a new monthly record of USD 134 bn in September. CPI inflation
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, mainly because of a 14.9% rise in
vehicle fuel prices. Food prices continued to fall (-1.7% y/y) and core

inflation was stable at 1%. Producer price inflation accelerated to 5%
(the highest since 2022) due to rising commodity prices. Growth in to-
tal domestic credit outstanding (“social financing”) continued to slow
down, reaching a record low of 7.1% y/y in September. Bank loans to
households declined (-2% y/y) for the seventh consecutive month.
In Q3 2026, real GDP growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y (1.2% q/q) from
4.3% y/y (0.9% q/q), primarily led by industrial production (which rose by

5.1% y/y on average in Q3 vs. 4.6% in Q2). On the demand side, exports
were the key driver while fixed-asset investment continued to contract.
Retail sales posted a small pickup in September but remained flat
in real terms in Q3 as a whole. With growth reaching 4.6% y/y year-to-
date, China needs only a 4.2% growth in Q4 to meet the official target of
4.5%-5% for this year – which seems easily feasible. The IMF trimmed
its growth forecast by 0.1pp to 4.5% in 2026 and 4% in 2027.

Indonesia
MSCI launched a consultation on reclassifying the country from Emer-
ging Market to Standalone Market status, with a final decision due by
end-June 2027, citing opaque shareholding structures and suspected
coordinating trading. Since January 2026, MSCI had frozen Index ad -
justments and threatened only a downgrade to Frontier market status
(like Pakistan), but market participants argued that a Frontier reclas -
sification would not properly reflect the extent of the market’s pro -

blems. Over the medium term, the risk is heavy foreign capital outflows
at a time when net FDI no longer covers the current account deficit.
This leaves Indonesia reliant on foreign portfolio inflows and the ru -
piah exposed to a reversal in sentiment. On the day of this announce -
ment, the Jakarta Composite Index fell by 11.6%, its steepest drop since
January 1998. Among Asian emerging markets, Indonesia has posted
the worst stock market performance since the beginning of the year

(-40.7%).
COMMODITIES
Iran/Oil. More of the same. Brent oil prices have remained broadly
stable this week (0.5% at 107$/b on Monday). Renewed Iranian at -
tacks on tankers in the Strait and intensification of Houthis’ attack over
the Saudi territory feed the geopolitical risk premium on barrel prices.
But IEA announcement of an accelerated 100 mb oil release (above
the March initial target of 400 mb) and reports that the US may lift
sanctions on the Russian and Iranian shadow fleets have contributed to

crude price stability. The spread with physical barrel (Dated Brent) has
declined (6.7$/b on Oct 16th). Trump administration’s various attempts
to lower diesel prices have had a limited impact for now. European gas
prices (TTF) recorded another weekly rise (4.7% w/w to 88.4 €/MWh,
the highest level since Dec 22nd, 2022) on the back of the rising number
of attacks on vessels in the Gulf and persisting disruption in the Strait.
However, EU gas inventories reached 74% of full capacities on October

16th (i.e., almost at the 80% month-end target), which should reduce
support for TTF prices going forward.
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels

## Document 285: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

投資焦點美國公債
► 美國財政部 9月10日執行10年期與20年期公
債回購計畫，並擴大回購規模從20億增至60
億美元 ，欲對缺乏 流動性的長天期舊債進行
流動性支持 ，減輕公債拋售 潮引發期限 溢價
走升問題 ，然實際回購規模僅 51.8億美元並
低於預期的 60億至100億美元令市場失望 。
財政部亦公布例行 10年與30年期公債標售結
果，宣布執行擴大回購操作後 ，從投標倍數
與得標 減預期利率 差距來看 ，反映長 天期公
債需求不淡 ，然川普承諾 共和黨若在期中選
舉中獲勝 ，將向全國成年 公民發錢，恐導致
未來公債 供給量續升 ，後續視 美國政府能否

維持財政紀律逐步恢復市場信心。
► 美國非農新增就業數明顯較前月改善 ，反映
就業市 場韌 性 ， 體現 實體 經濟表 現優 於 預
期，然短期 通膨難 回政策目標，預期聯準會
最快9月重啟升息 ，2年期公債利率水準已反
映升息空間 ，考量財政問題 與美國升 息預期
同在 ，投資美國公債宜慎 ，留意Fed官員態
度與經濟預估變化。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 美國經濟未見明顯疲態！為聯準會埋下升息伏筆財政部倍增長期公債回購不如預期，然標售需求回溫
美財政部倍增公債回購不如預期，保守看待公債配置

投資焦點
► 2026年第2季美國標普500企業財報數據顯示
獲利成長明顯高於債務增速 ，另可發現代表
獲利護城河的大型企業毛利率貼近 2022年以
來高位，淨利率亦創1998年以來新高 ，資本
支出年增至 25%歷史高位水準 ，另資本 支出
佔EBITDA比率升至 35%，預期美國大型企
業持續擴大資本 支出推動獲利 成長動能 ，同
時維持獲利高於資本開支的資本效率 ，避免
大額資本支出過度失控 ，造成未來債信體質
與償債能力惡化。
► 從今年1月至8月美國各天期投資級債表現來
看，可以發現中短天期債含息總報酬仍維持
正值，主因美國政府財政 問題與升息擔憂推
升殖利率曲線上行 。從短、中、長天期殖利
率/存續期間比率來看 ，美國短天期債具備利
率波動下最大抗震能力 ，而長天期承受 利率
上行耐受幅度相對僅 50個基本點 ，考量利率
風險可能持續至年底 ，債市投資人可配置中
短天期債券降擾動。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 美國中短天期投資級債年至今表現相對較穩美國大型企業淨利率創1998年來新高

美國大型企業債信仍強，中短天期投資級債為首選

### Page 8

8
股市
 全球主要國家債務攀升 ，利率創高添股市評價修正風險 ，加上科技股舉債投入資本支出 ，未來獲利
與變現能力備受關注 。美伊衝突加劇不確定性 ，短線可透過布局醫療與公用事業等防禦股 ，降低市
場震盪風險 ，科技及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利等基本面支持長線發展 ，採逢回分批布局 ，並
分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，日本半導體股
分批布局較穩健，歐洲國防軍工仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融、工業等產業機會
區域：日本銀行股、日本半導體股、歐洲
金融、國防
債市
 發債供給與聯準會升息預期 ，美國公債殖利率續升，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國10
年期公債利率將於4.5%-5.0%震盪，可逢利率走升時 ，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體
質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者 ，包含金融、科技、通訊及公

用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會9月升息預期持續，美元短線高檔震盪。
 日本銀行釋出鷹派訊號，9月升息可能性升高，有利日圓止貶回升。
日圓震盪偏升
商品
 中東衝突反覆 ，荷姆茲海峽短期恢復正常通行機率偏低 ，加上石油庫存探低 ，預期今年能源仍呈供
不應求態勢，油價維持偏高水位。
 美國財務壓力與去美元化之兩個長期結構性問題依然存在，央行買盤提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金區間操作，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 債務供給與升息預期擾動，基本面形塑中長期布局契機

### Page 15

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

WEEKLY AGGREGATES
ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_874
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.
